People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um, relationships? Oh, no, not at all. My friends and family are great, really. I mean, they know I can be a little forgetful sometimes, like maybe forgetting to call back right away or mixing up a dinner time, but they don't mind. They know I care about them!

Um, careless mistakes? Oh, I really hope nobody thinks I'm careless. I wouldn't use that word at all, no. I take a lot of pride in my work! I always try my absolute best to do things right the first time. 

I mean, sure, I guess sometimes a tiny detail might slip by... like maybe glancing at a work order and going to apartment 3B instead of 3D, or maybe forgetting to log a part I used right away. But that's just because we have so many maintenance tickets to get through every day, you know? I really do care, a whole lot.

Restless? Oh, no, I wouldn't say restless. I mean, I'm just a really energetic person! I like to keep moving.

Disorganized? Oh, gosh, no, I definitely wouldn't use that word! That makes it sound like I'm messy or don't care, and I really, really do care about keeping things nice. 

I mean, I guess my tool belt can get a little... cluttered sometimes? But it's just because I want to make sure I have absolutely everything I might need for any job that comes up! It's better to be over-prepared than under-prepared, right? And sure, sometimes my paperwork for the maintenance logs gets a little piled up on the passenger seat of my truck, but I always know exactly where everything is. It's more of an... organized chaos, you know? 

I always make sure the important things get turned in eventually. I try so hard to keep on top of it all. As long as the repairs get done and the tenants are happy, a little bit of clutter in my truck isn't really a big deal, is it? I think my boss knows my heart is in the right place.

Everyone gets a little busy and distracted sometimes. That's... that's normal, isn't it? I just wouldn't want anyone to think I'm a bad friend or a bad sister just because I forget a text message here and there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any impact on your daily life or overall well-being due to these forgetfulness issues?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I always think it's better to just put on a smile and try harder the next day. That's the right attitude to have, don't you think? I'm sure my boss prefers someone who stays upbeat rather than someone who gets all mopey over a little slip-up, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're trying to focus on your work?\n\nPatient: Restless? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Focus? Well, I wouldn't say I have *difficulty*... I mean, I can focus just fine when I'm fixing a leaky pipe or patching drywall! I'm really good at the hands-on stuff. 

But I guess... well, with the new digital work orders on the tablet... I try really hard to read all the notes the office puts in there. I'll start reading, thinking I've got it all figured out, but then... I don't know, maybe a door shuts down the hall, or I start thinking about the next unit I have to go to, and suddenly I'm halfway down the screen and I realize I didn't actually take in a single word I just looked at. 

But that's just because I'm so eager to get to the actual fixing part, you know? Reading on a screen is just a little dry, isn't it? I'm sure lots of people drift off a little when they're reading boring paperwork, right? It doesn't mean I'm not paying attention to the important things, does it?

Hard to follow instructions? Oh, I always want to do exactly what's asked of me! I really try to be a good listener. I mean, I guess my house isn't always perfectly tidy, but it's nothing major. I definitely don't have the same kind of trouble at home that I've been having with the new digital system at work. I'm doing okay outside of the job... that's the right answer, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any problems in your relationships with family or friends due to your memory or follow-through?\n\nPatient: Um, relationships? Oh, no, not at all. My friends and family are great, really. I mean, they know I can be a little forgetful sometimes, like maybe forgetting to call back right away or mixing up a dinner time, but they don't mind. They know I care about them! \n\nI always make sure to apologize if I do mess up a plan, and I try to make it up to them. Nobody's perfect, right? So, yeah, no big problems there at all. Everyone gets a little busy and distracted sometimes. That's... that's normal, isn't it? I just wouldn't want anyone to think I'm a bad friend or a bad sister just because I forget a text message here and there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any impact on your daily life or overall well-being due to these forgetfulness issues?\n\nPatient: Um, well... Just a little bit. \n\nI've gotten a bit behind on things, so there's a... a backlog. And, um, a couple of the tenants might have mentioned something about work orders taking too long. I'm really scared, honestly. I've been working so hard for a promotion, and I'm terrified this is going to ruin my chances. I just want to do a good job, you know? I'm not a bad worker, I promise. It's just this new system... do you think it's understandable that I'm struggling a little bit with all these changes?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed about your situation at work?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious or depressed? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm a pretty positive person overall! I try to keep a good attitude, you know? \n\nI guess I've just been a little... stressed? But everyone gets stressed at work, right? Especially when they introduce a whole new computer system and expect you to learn it overnight. It's just a lot to take in. But I'm definitely not depressed or anything like that. I still get up every day and try my best. \n\nI just... I just really want to do a good job and get that promotion. So maybe I worry about it a little more than I should. But that's just because I care about my work, right? Oh, I really hope nobody thinks I'm careless. I wouldn't use that word at all, no. I take a lot of pride in my work! I always try my absolute best to do things right the first time. \n\nI mean, sure, I guess sometimes a tiny detail might slip by... like maybe glancing at a work order and going to apartment 3B instead of 3D, or maybe forgetting to log a part I used right away. But that's just because we have so many maintenance tickets to get through every day, you know? I really do care, a whole lot. \n\nIf I ever do miss a little detail like that, I always make sure to go right back and fix it the second I realize. I never just leave a job half-done on purpose. That shows I'm dedicated, right? As long as I go back and make it right, it shouldn't really count as a careless mistake, should it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself when you forget something or make a mistake at work?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... no, I really try not to do that. I mean, I feel a little bad if I inconvenience someone, of course, because I want everyone to be happy! But I don't sit around criticizing myself or anything. \n\nI just... I tell myself to shake it off and fix whatever went wrong. Being negative doesn't really help anything, does it? I always think it's better to just put on a smile and try harder the next day. That's the right attitude to have, don't you think? I'm sure my boss prefers someone who stays upbeat rather than someone who gets all mopey over a little slip-up, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're trying to focus on your work?\n\nPatient: Restless? That's the best way to handle things, don't you think?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your mind wanders or you get distracted easily when you're supposed to be focusing on a task?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 49 / maintenance_or_repair_worker_general / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Mind wandering? Oh, well... I wouldn't say I get *easily* distracted. I mean, I really do try to stay on task, especially when I'm working on something important for a tenant. 

I guess... sometimes if I'm, say, waiting for a sealant to dry, or if I'm just doing a routine filter change that I've done a million times... maybe my thoughts drift a little bit? Like, I might start thinking about what I need to pick up at the grocery store on the way home, or wondering if I left the porch light on. But that's just because my hands already know exactly what to do, you know? It's like autopilot. 

I always get the job done, though! I'm sure everyone's mind wanders a little bit when they're doing something repetitive, right? It's perfectly normal to have a few stray thoughts when you're just working quietly by yourself, isn't it?

I'm doing okay outside of the job... that's the right answer, isn't it? Oh, no, not at all. My friends and family are great, really. I mean, they know I can be a little forgetful sometimes, like maybe forgetting to call back right away or mixing up a dinner time, but they don't mind. They know I care about them! \n\nI always make sure to apologize if I do mess up a plan, and I try to make it up to them. Nobody's perfect, right? So, yeah, no big problems there at all. Everyone gets a little busy and distracted sometimes. That's... that's normal, isn't it? I just wouldn't want anyone to think I'm a bad friend or a bad sister just because I forget a text message here and there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any impact on your daily life or overall well-being due to these forgetfulness issues?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or anything. I know I just need to try harder. But... it is starting to become a bit of an issue at work. But I'm going to catch up, I really am! It's just... well, there was also a safety review on my record recently because of a step I missed in the new system. \n\nI'm just... I'm really scared, honestly. I've been working so hard for a promotion, and I'm terrified this is going to ruin my chances. I just want to do a good job, you know? I'm not a bad worker, I promise. It's just this new system... do you think it's understandable that I'm struggling a little bit with all these changes?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed about your situation at work?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious or depressed? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm a pretty positive person overall! I try to keep a good attitude, you know? \n\nI guess I've just been a little... stressed? But everyone gets stressed at work, right? Especially when they introduce a whole new computer system and expect you to learn it overnight. It's just a lot to take in. But I'm definitely not depressed or anything like that. I still get up every day and try my best. \n\nI just... I just really want to do a good job and get that promotion. So maybe I worry about it a little more than I should. But that's just because I care about my work, right? It's good to care about your job, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work?\n\nPatient: Um, careless mistakes? Oh, I really hope nobody thinks I'm careless. I wouldn't use that word at all, no. I take a lot of pride in my work! I always try my absolute best to do things right the first time. \n\nI mean, sure, I guess sometimes a tiny detail might slip by... like maybe glancing at a work order and going to apartment 3B instead of 3D, or maybe forgetting to log a part I used right away. But that's just because we have so many maintenance tickets to get through every day, you know? I really do care, a whole lot. \n\nIf I ever do miss a little detail like that, I always make sure to go right back and fix it the second I realize. I never just leave a job half-done on purpose. That shows I'm dedicated, right? As long as I go back and make it right, it shouldn't really count as a careless mistake, should it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself when you forget something or make a mistake at work?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... no, I really try not to do that. I mean, I feel a little bad if I inconvenience someone, of course, because I want everyone to be happy! But I don't sit around criticizing myself or anything. \n\nI just... I tell myself to shake it off and fix whatever went wrong. Being negative doesn't really help anything, does it? I always think it's better to just put on a smile and try harder the next day. That's the right attitude to have, don't you think? I'm sure my boss prefers someone who stays upbeat rather than someone who gets all mopey over a little slip-up, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're trying to focus on your work?\n\nPatient: Restless? Oh, no, I wouldn't say restless. I mean, I'm just a really energetic person! I like to keep moving. \n\nWhen you do maintenance, you're always on your feet anyway, fixing a sink here or changing a light fixture there. So I'm used to being active. If I'm sitting down trying to figure out that new computer system for the work orders, well... maybe I bounce my knee a little, or tap my pen? But that's just because I'm eager to get back out there and do the actual hands-on work. It means I'm a hard worker, right? \n\nI don't think a little extra energy is a bad thing at all. It helps me get through all the tickets we have in a day. It's good to have energy for your job, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you consider yourself a disorganized person?\n\nPatient: Disorganized? Oh, gosh, no, I definitely wouldn't use that word! That makes it sound like I'm messy or don't care, and I really, really do care about keeping things nice. \n\nI mean, I guess my tool belt can get a little... cluttered sometimes? But it's just because I want to make sure I have absolutely everything I might need for any job that comes up! It's better to be over-prepared than under-prepared, right? And sure, sometimes my paperwork for the maintenance logs gets a little piled up on the passenger seat of my truck, but I always know exactly where everything is. It's more of an... organized chaos, you know? \n\nI always make sure the important things get turned in eventually. I try so hard to keep on top of it all. As long as the repairs get done and the tenants are happy, a little bit of clutter in my truck isn't really a big deal, is it? I think my boss knows my heart is in the right place.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks for long periods of time?\n\nPatient: Focus? Well, I wouldn't say I have *difficulty*... I mean, I can focus just fine when I'm fixing a leaky pipe or patching drywall! I'm really good at the hands-on stuff. \n\nBut I guess... well, with the new digital work orders on the tablet... I try really hard to read all the notes the office puts in there. I'll start reading, thinking I've got it all figured out, but then... I don't know, maybe a door shuts down the hall, or I start thinking about the next unit I have to go to, and suddenly I'm halfway down the screen and I realize I didn't actually take in a single word I just looked at. \n\nBut that's just because I'm so eager to get to the actual fixing part, you know? And then I just have to ask him to say the rest of it again. But that's just because I want to make absolutely sure I don't mess up his instructions, right? I just go back and fix it as soon as I realize! There's really no use getting all worked up over a little slip of the mind, is there? I just apologize, if I need to, and make it right. Overwhelmed? Oh, well... I wouldn't use a strong word like overwhelmed. I mean, I try to handle everything with a smile, you know? I really do. 

But... I guess... since you're asking about my daily life... it's just that the work situation is getting a little more serious than I'd like to admit. Like I mentioned, there's that backlog of orders, and those few tenant complaints... and then that safety review going on my record. It's just... it's a lot all at once. 

I'm just so worried because I've been putting in so much effort, you know? I really, really want that promotion I've been working toward, and I'm just so scared that all these little slip-ups are going to cost me my chance. I mean, it's normal to feel a little stressed when you're trying to move up in your career, isn't it? I just want my boss to know I'm trying my absolute best. That counts for something, right?

It's just this new system... do you think it's understandable that I'm struggling a little bit with all these changes?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed about your situation at work?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious or depressed? Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I'm a pretty positive person overall! I try to keep a good attitude, you know? \n\nI guess I've just been a little... stressed? But everyone gets stressed at work, right? Especially when they introduce a whole new computer system and expect you to learn it overnight. It's just a lot to take in. But I'm definitely not depressed or anything like that. I still get up every day and try my best. \n\nI just... I just really want to do a good job and get that promotion. So maybe I worry about it a little more than I should. But that's just because I care about my work, right? It's good to care about your job, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work?\n\nPatient: Um, careless mistakes? Oh, I really hope nobody thinks I'm careless. I wouldn't use that word at all, no. I take a lot of pride in my work! I always try my absolute best to do things right the first time. \n\nI mean, sure, I guess sometimes a tiny detail might slip by... like maybe glancing at a work order and going to apartment 3B instead of 3D, or maybe forgetting to log a part I used right away. But that's just because we have so many maintenance tickets to get through every day, you know? I really do care, a whole lot. \n\nIf I ever do miss a little detail like that, I always make sure to go right back and fix it the second I realize. I never just leave a job half-done on purpose. That shows I'm dedicated, right? As long as I go back and make it right, it shouldn't really count as a careless mistake, should it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or criticize yourself when you forget something or make a mistake at work?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... no, I really try not to do that. I mean, I feel a little bad if I inconvenience someone, of course, because I want everyone to be happy! But I don't sit around criticizing myself or anything. \n\nI just... I tell myself to shake it off and fix whatever went wrong. Being negative doesn't really help anything, does it? I always think it's better to just put on a smile and try harder the next day. That's the right attitude to have, don't you think? I'm sure my boss prefers someone who stays upbeat rather than someone who gets all mopey over a little slip-up, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're trying to focus on your work?\n\nPatient: Restless? Oh, no, I wouldn't say restless. I mean, I'm just a really energetic person! I like to keep moving. \n\nWhen you do maintenance, you're always on your feet anyway, fixing a sink here or changing a light fixture there. So I'm used to being active. If I'm sitting down trying to figure out that new computer system for the work orders, well... maybe I bounce my knee a little, or tap my pen? But that's just because I'm eager to get back out there and do the actual hands-on work. It means I'm a hard worker, right? \n\nI don't think a little extra energy is a bad thing at all. It helps me get through all the tickets we have in a day. It's good to have energy for your job, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you consider yourself a disorganized person?\n\nPatient: Disorganized? Oh, gosh, no, I definitely wouldn't use that word! That makes it sound like I'm messy or don't care, and I really, really do care about keeping things nice. \n\nI mean, I guess my tool belt can get a little... cluttered sometimes? But it's just because I want to make sure I have absolutely everything I might need for any job that comes up! It's better to be over-prepared than under-prepared, right? And sure, sometimes my paperwork for the maintenance logs gets a little piled up on the passenger seat of my truck, but I always know exactly where everything is. It's more of an... organized chaos, you know? \n\nI always make sure the important things get turned in eventually. I try so hard to keep on top of it all. As long as the repairs get done and the tenants are happy, a little bit of clutter in my truck isn't really a big deal, is it? I think my boss knows my heart is in the right place.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks for long periods of time?\n\nPatient: Focus? Well, I wouldn't say I have *difficulty*... I mean, I can focus just fine when I'm fixing a leaky pipe or patching drywall! I'm really good at the hands-on stuff. \n\nBut I guess... well, with the new digital work orders on the tablet... I try really hard to read all the notes the office puts in there. I'll start reading, thinking I've got it all figured out, but then... I don't know, maybe a door shuts down the hall, or I start thinking about the next unit I have to go to, and suddenly I'm halfway down the screen and I realize I didn't actually take in a single word I just looked at. \n\nBut that's just because I'm so eager to get to the actual fixing part, you know? Reading on a screen is just a little dry, isn't it? I'm sure lots of people drift off a little when they're reading boring paperwork, right? It doesn't mean I'm not paying attention to the important things, does it?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to follow instructions or remember them for more than a few minutes?\n\nPatient: Hard to follow instructions? Oh, I always want to do exactly what's asked of me! I really try to be a good listener. It's just... well, sometimes if my supervisor is talking straight to me, giving me a rundown on a repair or an audit... I'll be looking right at him, giving him my full respect, you know? \n\nBut then... I guess maybe only the first part of what he says really sticks. And then I just have to ask him to say the rest of it again. But that's just because I want to make absolutely sure I don't mess up his instructions, right? It's always better to ask twice and do the repair right than to guess and do it wrong. That just shows I care about doing a good job for him, doesn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you realize you've forgotten something important at work?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Oh, gosh, no, I wouldn't say I get anxious! I try really, really hard to keep a positive attitude on the job. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm, you know, a nervous wreck or anything. \n\nI mean, if I do happen to leave a tool behind in a unit or maybe forget to check off a box on the digital form... I just go back and fix it as soon as I realize! There's really no use getting all worked up over a little slip of the mind, is there? I just apologize, if I need to, and make it right. I think my boss really appreciates that I stay calm and just focus on doing good work. That's the best way to handle things, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders or you get distracted easily when you're supposed to be focusing on a task?\n\nPatient: Mind wandering? Oh, well... I wouldn't say I get *easily* distracted. I mean, I really do try to stay on task, especially when I'm working on something important for a tenant. \n\nI guess... sometimes if I'm, say, waiting for a sealant to dry, or if I'm just doing a routine filter change that I've done a million times... maybe my thoughts drift a little bit? Like, I might start thinking about what I need to pick up at the grocery store on the way home, or wondering if I left the porch light on. But that's just because my hands already know exactly what to do, you know? It's like autopilot. \n\nI always get the job done, though! I'm sure everyone's mind wanders a little bit when they're doing something repetitive, right? It's perfectly normal to have a few stray thoughts when you're just working quietly by yourself, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Have you had to cut back on any social activities or responsibilities outside of work due to your memory or attention problems?\n\nPatient: Oh, social activities? No, no, I wouldn't say I've had to cut back on anything like that! I mean, I still see my friends and keep up with my family just fine. Oh, um... I really wouldn't say I'm *not* paying attention. I actually pay very close attention to making sure the tenants are taken care of! 

Like, I’ll begin a maintenance job, and I always make sure to do the actual fix. I get the physical work done, and I do it well, I really do! But... well, I guess if I'm looking at where things get a little messy... it's just that somehow I end up leaving the last little steps undone. 

Just the minor things, really! I think once the actual hands-on repair is working, my brain just kind of jumps to the next person who needs my help, you know? 

So I don't think it's that I don't care or aren't paying attention. I just want to be as helpful as possible to everyone. And getting the actual fix done is the most important part anyway, isn't it? As long as their apartment is working, the paperwork can wait a little bit, right?
